RF field accumulation, visualization overlays, and HTTP streaming endpoints.

## `config.py`
8,168 bytes; text; SHA-256 `1c839f23790e969f1e357c5757c36f23a01d06c2a9f63b1198b3d39d82105b76`

Central configuration for the WiFi signal visualizer project.

Hardware target: Raspberry Pi 5 (16 GB RAM, quad-core Cortex-A76 @ 2.4 GHz).
Edit this file to tune camera, WiFi scanning, signal processing,
RF field behaviour, and server settings.

```text
"""Central configuration for the WiFi signal visualizer project.

Hardware target: Raspberry Pi 5 (16 GB RAM, quad-core Cortex-A76 @ 2.4 GHz).
Edit this file to tune camera, WiFi scanning, signal processing,
RF field behaviour, and server settings.
"""

# ── Camera ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Pi 5 targets: ≥ 25 FPS at 1280×720 on V3 Camera / USB UVC camera
CAMERA_INDEX = 0
CAMERA_WIDTH = 1280              # Pi 5: 1280×720 (was 640×480 on Pi Zero 2W)
CAMERA_HEIGHT = 720
```

function [groundtruthall,all_sequenceName] = benchmark_groundtruth(cls,path2gt,path2testAll, cls_mapping)
        % get the ground truth box for this class
        try 
            a = load(fullfile(path2gt,'groundtruth.mat'));
        catch
            path2gt = '/n/fs/modelnet/SUN3DV2/prepareGT/Metadata/';
            a = load(fullfile(path2gt,'groundtruth.mat'));
        end
            
        if ~isempty(cls)
            for i = 1:length(a.groundtruth)
                index_mapping = find(ismember(cls_mapping.Name_6585, a.groundtruth(i).classname));
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/cooperative_scene_parsing/evaluation/detection/computePRCurve3D.m`
3,221 bytes; text; SHA-256 `ff6d4aa67cb7e0704779ca7251194d580631940efdb0c36b51b087e41e7b6c29`

```text
function [apScore,precision,reccall,isTp,isFp,isMissed,gtAssignment,maxOverlaps,numOfgt,gtIdxAll,allOverlaps] ...
    = computePRCurveTightBB(classname,predictedBbsTight,imageIds,groundTruthBbs,isDifficult)
    if length(groundTruthBbs) ~= length(isDifficult), error('inconsistent difficulty size.'); end
    if size(groundTruthBbs,1) ==1,groundTruthBbs= groundTruthBbs';end
    if size(isDifficult,1) ==1,isDifficult= isDifficult';end
    
    gtAssignment = zeros(length(predictedBbsTight),1);
    
    % pick ground truth of current class
    isMissed = false(numel(groundTruthBbs),1);
    isSameClass = ismember({groundTruthBbs.classname},classname);
    numOfgt =sum(isSameClass);
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/cooperative_scene_parsing/evaluation/detection/get_average_precision.m`
807 bytes; text; SHA-256 `17025c97ed05996d1b71929766f6ef135e7af697b5bce4076ad0ef51a5d4a1a4`

```text
% Returns the average precision score given precision and recall vectors. The AP is computed by
% numerically integrating the area under the PR curve.
%
% Note: this code was taken from the VOC2011 toolkit.
%
% Args:
%   precision - Px1 vector of precision scores, where P is the number of predictions. Note that the
%               precision scores must be monotonically decreasing.
%   recall - Px1 vector of recall scores, where P is the number of predictions.
%
% Returns:
%   ap - the average precision.
```

```text
function sequenceName = getSequenceName(thispath,dataRoot)
    if ~exist('dataRoot','var'),
        dataRoot = '/n/fs/sun3d/data/';
    end
    sequenceName  = thispath(length(dataRoot):end);
    while sequenceName(1)=='/',sequenceName =sequenceName(2:end);end
    while sequenceName(end)=='/',sequenceName =sequenceName(1:end-1);end 
end
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/cooperative_scene_parsing/evaluation/SUNRGBDtoolbox/mBB/cuboidVolume.m`
157 bytes; text; SHA-256 `e8dd89540d2e4e24eafa23f5c0e2ca50359353f433585e17c9367c761347f587`

```text
function volume = cuboidVolume(bb)

dis = (bb([1 2 5 6],:)-bb([3 4 3 4],:)).^2;

volume = (bb(10,:)-bb(9,:)).*sqrt((dis(1,:)+dis(2,:)).*(dis(3,:)+dis(4,:)));
```

```text
function [points2d,z3] = project3dPtsTo2d(points3d,Rtilt,crop,K)
    %% inverse of get_aligned_point_cloud
    points3d =[Rtilt'*points3d']';
    
    %% inverse rgb_plane2rgb_world
    
    
    % Now, swap Y and Z.
    points3d(:, [2, 3]) = points3d(:,[3, 2]);
    
    % Make the original consistent with the camera location:
    x3 = points3d(:,1);
```

function [bb2d,bb2dDraw] = projectStructBbsTo2d(bb,Rtilt,crop,K)

       crop =[1,1];
    end
    if isempty(bb),
        bb2d =[];
        bb2dDraw =[];

        nBbs = numel(bb);
        if isfield(bb,'confidence'),
            conf = [bb.confidence];
            conf = conf(:);
```

****************************************************************************************
Data: Image depth and label data are in SUNRGBD.zip
image: rgb image
depth: depth image  to read the depth see the code in SUNRGBDtoolbox/read3dPoints/.
extrinsics: the rotation matrix to align the point could with gravity
fullres: full resolution depth and rgb image
intrinsics.txt  : sensor intrinsic
scene.txt  : scene type
annotation2Dfinal  : 2D segmentation
annotation3Dfinal  : 3D bounding box
annotation3Dlayout : 3D room layout bounding box

```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/cooperative_scene_parsing/evaluation/vis/draw/drawRoom.m`
957 bytes; text; SHA-256 `76287ac292ee792b8cdba8797f44ca564805199c17ab8525f942a4f72bf6a7d7`

```text
function drawRoom(roomLayout,color,lineWidth,maxhight,lineStyle)
totalPoints  = size(roomLayout,2);
bottompoints= roomLayout(:,1:totalPoints/2);
toppoints = roomLayout(:,totalPoints/2+1:totalPoints);
toppoints(3,toppoints(3,:)>maxhight)=maxhight;
bottompoints(3,bottompoints(3,:)>maxhight)=maxhight;
[~,ind] = min(toppoints(2,:));
for i =1:length(toppoints)-1
%     if i~=ind&&i+1~=ind
    vis_line(toppoints(:,i)', toppoints(:,i+1)', color, lineWidth, lineStyle);
    vis_line(bottompoints(:,i)', bottompoints(:,i+1)', color, lineWidth, lineStyle);
    vis_line(toppoints(:,i)', bottompoints(:,i)', color, lineWidth, lineStyle);
```

function plotcube(varargin)
% PLOTCUBE - Display a 3D-cube in the current axes
%
%   PLOTCUBE(EDGES,ORIGIN,ALPHA,COLOR) displays a 3D-cube in the current axes
%   with the following properties:
%   * EDGES : 3-elements vector that defines the length of cube edges
%   * ORIGIN: 3-elements vector that defines the start point of the cube
%   * ALPHA : scalar that defines the transparency of the cube faces (from 0
%             to 1)
%   * COLOR : 3-elements vector that defines the faces color of the cube
%
% Example:
```

% Visualizes a 3D point cloud.
%
% Args:
%   points3d - Nx3 or Nx2 point cloud where N is the number of points.
%   colors - (optional) Nx3 vector of colors or Nx1 vector of values which which
%            be scaled for visualization.

function sequenceName = getSequenceName(thispath,dataRoot)

```text
function volume = cuboidVolume(bb)

dis = (bb([1 2 5 6],:)-bb([3 4 3 4],:)).^2;

volume = (bb(10,:)-bb(9,:)).*sqrt((dis(1,:)+dis(2,:)).*(dis(3,:)+dis(4,:)));
```

function [points2d,z3] = project3dPtsTo2d(points3d,Rtilt,crop,K)
    %% inverse of get_aligned_point_cloud
    points3d =[Rtilt'*points3d']';
    
    %% inverse rgb_plane2rgb_world
    
    
    % Now, swap Y and Z.
    points3d(:, [2, 3]) = points3d(:,[3, 2]);
    
    % Make the original consistent with the camera location:
    x3 = points3d(:,1);
```

```text
function [rgb,points3d,depthInpaint,imsize]=read3dPoints(data)
         depthVis = imread(data.depthpath);
         imsize = size(depthVis);
         depthInpaint = bitor(bitshift(depthVis,-3), bitshift(depthVis,16-3));
         depthInpaint = single(depthInpaint)/1000; 
         depthInpaint(depthInpaint >8)=8;
         [rgb,points3d]=read_3d_pts_general(depthInpaint,data.K,size(depthInpaint),data.rgbpath);
         points3d = (data.Rtilt*points3d')';
end
```

    % imageName is the full path to image

    fx = K(1,1); fy = K(2,2); 
    invalid = depthInpaint==0;
    if ~isempty(imageName)
        im = imread(imageName);
        rgb = im2double(im);  
    else
        rgb =double(cat(3,zeros(depthInpaintsize(1),depthInpaintsize(2)),...
```

237 bytes; text; SHA-256 `47741fa243c22ab37d8809bbb5203b0b12b7fc4ba98dad50aa183bc92216482a`

```text
function fileStr = file2string(fname)
    fileStr = '';
    fid = fopen(fname,'r');
    tline = fgetl(fid);
    while ischar(tline)
        fileStr = [fileStr sprintf('\n') tline];
        tline = fgetl(fid);
    end
    fclose(fid);
end
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/HorizonNet/misc/panostretch.py`
5,688 bytes; text; SHA-256 `b0658c91e24de308c82d5d2fd7c5bf5799d3c74f5f77e51456ad6ff3252d38f0`

Functions: uv_meshgrid, _uv_tri, uv_tri, coorx2u, coory2v, u2coorx, v2coory, uv2xy, pano_connect_points, pano_stretch, visualize_pano_stretch

```text
import functools
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import map_coordinates


def uv_meshgrid(w, h):
    uv = np.stack(np.meshgrid(range(w), range(h)), axis=-1)
    uv = uv.astype(np.float64)
    uv[..., 0] = ((uv[..., 0] + 0.5) / w - 0.5) * 2 * np.pi
    uv[..., 1] = ((uv[..., 1] + 0.5) / h - 0.5) * np.pi
    return uv

```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/HorizonNet/preprocess.py`
3,121 bytes; text; SHA-256 `bf8fe65a2eed3a4ba0fec5194d6cfdfa0e9968363e8dedfd6237ce4cf54695d6`

This script preprocess the given 360 panorama image under euqirectangular projection
and dump them to the given directory for further layout prediction and visualization.
The script will:
    - extract and dump the vanishing points
    - rotate the equirect image to align with the detected VP
    - extract the VP aligned line segments (for further layout prediction model)
The dump files:
    - `*_VP.txt` is the vanishg points
    - `*_aligned_rgb.png` is the VP aligned RGB image
    - `*_aligned_line.png` is the VP aligned line segments images

Author: Cheng Sun
Email : chengsun@gapp.nthu.edu.tw

```text
'''
This script preprocess the given 360 panorama image under euqirectangular projection
and dump them to the given directory for further layout prediction and visualization.
The script will:
    - extract and dump the vanishing points
    - rotate the equirect image to align with the detected VP
    - extract the VP aligned line segments (for further layout prediction model)
The dump files:
    - `*_VP.txt` is the vanishg points
    - `*_aligned_rgb.png` is the VP aligned RGB image
    - `*_aligned_line.png` is the VP aligned line segments images

```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/HorizonNet/README.md`
10,452 bytes; text; SHA-256 `65a17dd985300e55028c0c182051eb2a52fe4177bf4d6e929a1c992387df84ce`

```text
# HorizonNet

This is the implementation of our CVPR'19 "[
HorizonNet: Learning Room Layout with 1D Representation and Pano Stretch Data Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.03861)" ([project page](https://sunset1995.github.io/HorizonNet/)).

**News, June 15, 2019** - Critical bug fix for general layout (`dataset.py`, `inference.py` and `misc/post_proc.py`)\
**News, Aug 19, 2019** - Report results on [Structured3D dataset](https://structured3d-dataset.org/). (See [the report :clipboard: on ST3D](README_ST3D.md)).

![](assets/teaser.jpg)

This repo is a **pure python** implementation that you can:
- **Inference on your images** to get cuboid or general shaped room layout
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/HorizonNet/README_PREPARE_DATASET.md`
2,070 bytes; text; SHA-256 `32a9cc61ba21cd2f5379122459d36480025bc34ca4edde58fc413c75d1299466`

```text
# How to prepare your dataset?

**TLDR;**
- Aligned images in `your_dataset/img/*.png` and ground truth in `your_dataset/label_cor/*.txt`
- Image in `1024 x 512` resolution
- Ground truth format:
    ```
    x_0 y_ceiling_0
    x_0 y_floor_0
    x_1 y_ceiling_1
    x_1 y_floor_1
    ...
```

4,447 bytes; text; SHA-256 `cadfec7f567b84a713820662ae93e7f50e9f63ac1e527add483769d47afdb612`

```text
# Results on Structured3D dataset

References:
- [Structured3D: A Large Photo-realistic Dataset for Structured 3D Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/1908.00222)
- [Structured3D project page](http://structured3d-dataset.org)
- [Structured3D github](https://github.com/bertjiazheng/Structured3D)

## Dataset preparation
- Please contact [Structured3D](http://structured3d-dataset.org) to get the datas.
- Download all zip files under `{YOUR_DIR}`. Don't extract them.
- Run below to extract rgb and layout with original furniture and lighting setting only:
    ```bash
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/headers/R2Shapes.header`
2,769 bytes; text; SHA-256 `0b76c6012ebf4977173e1a453e336a6385cb07672f105b95e0c4dfd809d40864`

```text
<HR><H2>Description:</H2>

The R2Shapes package supports basics 2D geometry classes.
It contains classes representing 2D primitives 
(such as vectors, points, etc.).  It also contains
classes for 2D transformations (such as 3x3 matrices, etc.) and 
higher level 2D shapes (such as polygons, boxes, circles, etc).  
<P>
For all pairs of 2D primitives (and many 2D shapes), functions are
provided for computing the relationships between them.  For instance,
there are functions to compute the distance between primitives
(R2Distance), check for intersections (R2Intersects), check whether
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/headers/R3Graphics.header`
1,389 bytes; text; SHA-256 `02410d62f36088f3507367ed49870a1b0d2737796001fc55fe2411186bddb3c3`

```text
<HR><H2>Description:</H2>

The R3Graphics package supports classes for basic computer graphics:
3D viewing, camera control, surface material properties, lighting, and scene graphs.
<P>


<HR><H2>List of Classes:</H2>

<DL>
<DT> Camera and viewport control:
<DL>
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/headers/R3Shapes.header`
3,561 bytes; text; SHA-256 `e05cc89a717e4e0de6552e1dac2afd4b8c769b4398c0580a06af025e1be793c4`

```text
<HR><H2>Description:</H2>

The R3Shapes package supports basics 3D geometry classes.
<P>
It contains classes representing 3D primitives (such as vectors,
points, lines, rays, spans, planes, and halfspaces).  It also contains
classes for 3D transformations (such as 4x4 matrices, etc.) and higher
level 3D shapes (such as polygons, boxes, spheres, cones, pyramids,
prisms, etc).  The higher level shape classes are derived from an
abstract R3Shape class which supports transformations, bounding box
queries, drawing, and other abstract geometric functions.
<P>
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/headers/RNBasics.header`
2,237 bytes; text; SHA-256 `67b011d181af57d05e17e0f079c123c35b59abdc0699891b3fffaaa14779de23`

```text
<HR><H2>Description:</H2>

The RNBasics package supports simple data types (RNScalar, RNInterval,
RNArray, RNQueue, RNHeap) and support several OS-dependent functions
(RNTime, RNMem, RNError).

The RNArray, RNQueue, and RNHeap classes are implemented as templates
that store collections of a specified data type (like STL classes).
The storage allocation for these containers is dynamic -- it expands
and contracts as entries are inserted and removed.  Once a container
has been constructed, entries and the data stored in them can be
accessed, rearranged, or modified via member functions or iteration
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/index.html`
1,953 bytes; text; SHA-256 `e904c9acb61617b8638d1984ce2b63ed70af3bf6646efa0d8949706f56b32d71`

```text
<HEAD>
<TITLE>GAPS Documentation</TITLE>
</HEAD>
<BODY>

<H1>GAPS</H1>

<P>GAPS is a software toolkit for geometric analysis of 3D scenes.</P>

<P>It contains the following four packages ...</P>
<UL>
<LI><A HREF="RNBasics.html">RNBasics</A> - Simple common stuff (e.g., memory allocation, error reporting, dynamic arrays, etc). 
```

```text
#! /bin/bash
# usage : makepkg name

# Create header
echo "<HTML>" > $1.html

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/docs/makepkg.awk`
1,473 bytes; text; SHA-256 `639eaf0df20aea95b22812d16edaf7f091a57123644bfe3dda7784381d477147`

```text
# Make html file with class documentation
# NOTE: Does not insert inherited members

$0~"class " && $0~"{" {
  # Parse package name and class name
  pkg_name = "RNFoobar";
  class_name = $2;

  # Print class name
  printf "<HR> <H2> <A NAME=\"%s\" HREF=\"../pkgs/%s/%s.h\">%s</A> </H2> \n\n",
    class_name, pkg_name, class_name, class_name;

```

 * we must exit back to the application.  On resumption, we reprocess
 * the marker.

 * tables for updating the shift register in one step with three exclusive-ors

in the text files, or lpng*.zip if you want DOS-style line endings.

ZLIB DATA COMPRESSION LIBRARY

zlib 1.2.5 is a general purpose data compression library.  All the code is
thread safe.  The data format used by the zlib library is described by RFCs
(Request for Comments) 1950 to 1952 in the files
http://www.ietf.org/rfc/rfc1950.txt (zlib format), rfc1951.txt (deflate format)
and rfc1952.txt (gzip format).

All functions of the compression library are documented in the file zlib.h
(volunteer to write man pages welcome, contact zlib@gzip.org).  A usage example
of the library is given in the file example.c which also tests that the library
is working correctly.  Another example is given in the file minigzip.c.  The
```

/*

The interface routines for reading and writing PLY polygon files.

Greg Turk, February 1994

A PLY file contains a single polygonal _object_.

vertices, faces, edges and materials.

Each type of element for a given object has one or more _properties_

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/pkgs/R3Shapes/R4Matrix.cpp`
20,142 bytes; text; SHA-256 `c843a291116cdd239abf27dee5cdf92c5a4d09dff9fa414549fbe5d420787058`

```text
/* Source file for the GAPS matrix class */

/*

NOTES:

Matrix concatenation order is designed to be compatible with OpenGL.
The idea is that transformations can be applied (matrices multiplied)
in the same order as OpenGL calls would be made.  Make more global
transformations first (i.e., ones at the top of the scene hierachy).
Vectors are column vectors on the right of matrices.  Transformation
matrices are post-multiplied.
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/README.txt`
1,442 bytes; text; SHA-256 `efffa699f32fcde52e19f340d078b8a33b3da386067e4ad92a9996b41ced5666`

```text
GAPS Users -

This directory contains all code for the GAPS software library.
There are several subdirectories:

    pkgs - source and include files for all packages (software libraries).
    apps - source files for several application and example programs. 
    makefiles - unix-style make file definitions
    vc - visual studio solution files
    lib - archive library (.lib) files (created during compilation).
    bin - executable files (created during compilation).

```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/gaps/vc/README.txt`
967 bytes; text; SHA-256 `10c836f3afc69ec5a694c745342a134f7c13ee14313e67ffcfc43fc93bc6bc54`

```text
To compile, you should be able to open vc.sln and "Rebuild Solution.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

This file contains notes about settings made when creating the vc.sln file.

All:
  General: Output Directory = $(SolutionDir)/$(ConfigurationName)/$(ProjectName)
  General: Intermediate Directory = $(SolutionDir)/$(ConfigurationName)/$(ProjectName)
  Input: Additional Dependencies = R3Graphics.lib R3Shapes.lib R2Shapes.lib RNBasics.lib jpeg.lib glut32.lib glu32.lib opengl32.lib
  C/C++: General: Additional Include Directories = ../../pkgs
  C/C++: Advanced: Comple as C++ Code (Set TP explicitly) --- except jpeg, png, fglut
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/representation/quadrics.py`
2,130 bytes; text; SHA-256 `edc8b7bc31485d6da4355712fc0b053262f932c6b530a1861363c1c962817d8c`

Functions: sample_quadric_surface, decode_covariance_roll_pitch_yaw, sample_cov_bf, compute_shape_element_influences

```text
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from external.ldif.util import camera_util


def sample_quadric_surface(quadric, center, samples):
    # Sample the quadric surfaces and the RBFs in world space, and composite them.
    # (ldif.representation.quadrics.sample_quadric_surface)
    samples = samples - center.unsqueeze(2)
    homogeneous_sample_coords = F.pad(samples, [0, 1], "constant", 1)
    half_distance = torch.matmul(quadric, homogeneous_sample_coords.transpose(-1, -2))
    half_distance = half_distance.transpose(-1, -2)
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/ldif/util/camera_util.py`
637 bytes; text; SHA-256 `3bfb3bc6e7469de3e54f37f2439c69224f77ed633bbe8cabd48d3ec3563d18a2`

Functions: roll_pitch_yaw_to_rotation_matrices

```text
import torch


def roll_pitch_yaw_to_rotation_matrices(roll_pitch_yaw):
    # Converts roll-pitch-yaw angles to rotation matrices.
    # (ldif.util.camera_util.roll_pitch_yaw_to_rotation_matrices)
    cosines = torch.cos(roll_pitch_yaw)
    sines = torch.sin(roll_pitch_yaw)
    cx, cy, cz = cosines.unbind(-1)
    sx, sy, sz = sines.unbind(-1)
    rotation = torch.stack(
        [cz * cy, cz * sy * sx - sz * cx, cz * sy * cx + sz * sx,
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/mesh_fusion/libfusioncpu/README.md`
1,188 bytes; text; SHA-256 `9b6c25009c3babdd2c8171c93846018275a3b757bdf82779bcdfbda8fcecb93d`

```text
# PyFusion

PyFusion is a Python framework for volumetric depth fusion.
It contains simple occupancy and TSDF fusion methods that can be executed on a CPU as well as on a GPU.

To use the code, first compile the native code via 

```bash
cd build
cmake ..
make
```
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/mesh_fusion/libfusiongpu/fusion_zach_tvl1.cu`
4,706 bytes; text; SHA-256 `96ef5b447437268dbbfb52164ef4ff4da323d8b8e674646b20b4b8a84e25fcf4`

```text
#include "gpu_common.h"

__global__ void kernel_zach_tvl1_dual(Volume u, Volume p, Volume hist, int vx_res3, float sigma) {
  CUDA_KERNEL_LOOP(idx, vx_res3) {
    int d,h,w;
    fusion_idx2dhw(idx, u.width_,u.height_, d,h,w);

    float u_curr = u.data_[idx];

    float u_x = u.data_[idx + (w < u.width_-1)] - u_curr;
    float u_y = u.data_[idx + (h < u.height_-1) * u.width_] - u_curr;
    float u_z = u.data_[idx + (d < u.depth_-1) * u.width_ * u.height_] - u_curr;
```

1,188 bytes; text; SHA-256 `9b6c25009c3babdd2c8171c93846018275a3b757bdf82779bcdfbda8fcecb93d`

```text
# PyFusion

PyFusion is a Python framework for volumetric depth fusion.
It contains simple occupancy and TSDF fusion methods that can be executed on a CPU as well as on a GPU.

To use the code, first compile the native code via 

```bash
cd build
cmake ..
make
```
```

1,939 bytes; text; SHA-256 `f176b9cd79a41a3459dc3c50eb013dc0063f4795729c4e1d0ff51adf093c14bb`

```text
========
PyMCubes
========

PyMCubes is an implementation of the marching cubes algorithm to extract
isosurfaces from volumetric data. The volumetric data can be given as a
three-dimensional NumPy array or as a Python function ``f(x, y, z)``. The first
option is much faster, but it requires more memory and becomes unfeasible for
very large volumes.

PyMCubes also provides a function to export the results of the marching cubes as
COLLADA ``(.dae)`` files. This requires the
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/mesh_fusion/README.md`
7,566 bytes; text; SHA-256 `d521a8a13a2535643879d24ba08e46717fadd66b310e85c97698ecdae6eeb1e3`

```text
# Watertight and Simplified Meshes through TSDF Fusion

This repository contains a simply Python pipeline for obtaining watertight
and simplified meshes from arbitrary triangular meshes, given in `.off` format.
The approach is largely based on adapted versions of Gernot Riegler's
[pyrender](https://github.com/griegler/pyrender) and [pyfusion](https://github.com/griegler/pyfusion);
it also uses [PyMCubes](https://github.com/pmneila/PyMCubes).

If you use any of this code, please make sure to cite the following work:

    @article{Stutz2018ARXIV,
        author    = {David Stutz and Andreas Geiger},
```

def c2e(cubemap, h, w, mode='bilinear', cube_format='dice'):

    elif mode == 'nearest':
        order = 0

```text
import numpy as np

from . import utils


def e2c(e_img, face_w=256, mode='bilinear', cube_format='dice'):
    '''
    e_img:  ndarray in shape of [H, W, *]
    face_w: int, the length of each face of the cubemap
    '''
    assert len(e_img.shape) == 3
    h, w = e_img.shape[:2]
```

```text
import numpy as np
from scipy.ndimage import map_coordinates


def xyzcube(face_w):
    '''
    Return the xyz cordinates of the unit cube in [F R B L U D] format.
    '''
    out = np.zeros((face_w, face_w * 6, 3), np.float32)
    rng = np.linspace(-0.5, 0.5, num=face_w, dtype=np.float32)
    grid = np.stack(np.meshgrid(rng, -rng), -1)

```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/py360convert/README.md`
4,853 bytes; text; SHA-256 `c06ae931810a36dc62bd450fd63f6fb977da4d6f79a61c513723c461e2f2be69`

```text
# py360convert

**I plan to refactor this project at the end of November but have no time to respond any questions before then.**

Features of this project:
- Convertion between cubemap and equirectangular  
    ![](assert/teaser_convertion.png)
- Equirectangular to planar  
    ![](assert/teaser_2planar.png)
- Pure python implementation and depend only on [numpy](http://www.numpy.org/) and [scipy](https://www.scipy.org/)
- Vectorization implementation (in most of the place)
    - `c2e` takes 300ms and `e2c` takes 160ms on 1.6 GHz Intel Core i5 CPU
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/external/pyTorchChamferDistance/README.md`
933 bytes; text; SHA-256 `d76c0175c7cfd815cef8792f7cca7d16006d91c7575de9d886f2f6c0d2baa1c6`

```text
# Chamfer Distance for pyTorch

This is an implementation of the Chamfer Distance as a module for pyTorch. It is written as a custom C++/CUDA extension.

As it is using pyTorch's [JIT compilation](https://pytorch.org/tutorials/advanced/cpp_extension.html), there are no additional prerequisite steps that have to be taken. Simply import the module as shown below; CUDA and C++ code will be compiled on the first run.

### Usage
```python
from chamfer_distance import ChamferDistance
chamfer_dist = ChamferDistance()

#...
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/models/multi_view/modules/embedding_net.py`
1,809 bytes; text; SHA-256 `0ac4ff0ecbd0d0ce6ad23790bbcd17c59f58550fb5724583e197290d75734b5d`

Classes: IDPredictor, Associative3DEmbedding

Functions: get_affinity_matrix

```text
import torch
from torch import nn as nn
from torch.nn import functional as F

from models.registers import MODULES
from models.pano3d.modules.scene_gcn import FeatureExtractor


def get_affinity_matrix(embedding1, embedding2):
    embedding1 = embedding1.unsqueeze(1).expand(-1, embedding2.shape[0], -1)
    embedding2 = embedding2.expand(embedding1.shape[0], -1, -1)
    affinity = F.cosine_similarity(embedding1, embedding2, dim=-1)
```

```text
from models.training import BaseTrainer

class Trainer(BaseTrainer):

    def eval_step(self, data):
        loss = self.compute_loss(data)
        loss['total'] = loss['total'].item()
        return loss

    def compute_loss(self, data):
        data = self.to_device(data)
        est_data = self.net(data)
```

## `DeepPanoContext(github-1)/DeepPanoContext-main/utils/net_utils.py`
408 bytes; text; SHA-256 `1a836859ca66f0782c8f352a7390783539515158c567892757a113509b5ccd8d`

Functions: weights_init

```text

#initialize the weighs of the network for Convolutional layers and batchnorm layers
def weights_init(m):
    classname = m.__class__.__name__
    if classname.find('Conv') != -1:
        m.weight.data.normal_(0.0, 0.02)
    elif classname.find('BatchNorm') != -1:
        if hasattr(m, 'weight'):
            m.weight.data.normal_(1.0, 0.02)
        if hasattr(m, 'bias'):
            m.bias.data.fill_(0)

```

## `depth_estimator.py`
11,837 bytes; text; SHA-256 `e192a18d4c1c16580b1a2d1e1bdd33ed99e1dca45973b1d09cb2caf6b5e8d3fe`

Monocular depth estimator using MiDaS-Small (ONNX, CPU-only).

Adapted from the depth-estimation concepts in DeepPanoContext (ICCV 2021)
and holistic_scene_parsing (ECCV 2018), but re-implemented as a lightweight
ONNX inference wrapper that runs on Raspberry Pi 5 without a GPU.

Performance target (Raspberry Pi 5): ≤ 200 ms per 1280×960 image.

Usage::

    estimator = DepthEstimator()
    depth_map = estimator.estimate(bgr_image)   # returns float32 HxW array

Classes: DepthEstimator

Indoor furniture detector using YOLOv8-nano (ONNX, CPU-only).

Inspired by the object detection stage of DeepPanoContext (ICCV 2021)
but replaced with a lightweight YOLOv8-nano model that runs on
Raspberry Pi 5 CPU without CUDA.

Performance target (Raspberry Pi 5): ≤ 150 ms per image.

Output per image::

    [
      {
        "label":      "chair",
        "confidence": 0.82,
        "bbox_2d":    [x1, y1, x2, y2],   # pixel coords in original image
        "class_id":   56,
      },
      ...
    ]

## `heatmap_3d_renderer.py`
19,812 bytes; text; SHA-256 `d430ac8906b2e4d92c96de2c7e5a0b97b4435a56426cdaaa4ade449444b960ee`

Ekahau-style 3D isometric RF heatmap renderer (matplotlib).

Reads:
  - A FloorPlan (from room_reconstructor.py)
  - Georeferenced RF samples (from RFDatabase or CSV)

Produces:
  - A high-resolution PNG matching the Ekahau Site Survey visual style:
      · 3D isometric floor plan with white walls
      · RF signal heatmap draped on the floor
        (Blue=very weak → Red → Orange → Yellow → Green=strong)
      · Walk trajectory line
      · AP location pins (blue, with label)
      · Professional colorbar (dBm scale)

Performance note: matplotlib 3D rendering takes ~5–15 s on Pi Zero 2W.
This is acceptable for an on-demand export endpoint.

Classes: Ekahau3DRenderer

Functions: _dbm_to_norm, _signal_colormap, _idw_grid, _fast_idw_grid, render_from_csv, main

```text
"""Ekahau-style 3D isometric RF heatmap renderer (matplotlib).

Reads:
  - A FloorPlan (from room_reconstructor.py)
  - Georeferenced RF samples (from RFDatabase or CSV)

Produces:
  - A high-resolution PNG matching the Ekahau Site Survey visual style:
      · 3D isometric floor plan with white walls
      · RF signal heatmap draped on the floor
        (Blue=very weak → Red → Orange → Yellow → Green=strong)
      · Walk trajectory line
```

## `imu_reader.py`
10,207 bytes; text; SHA-256 `6d6a42aad11db01760ddc01bf778c118fd4dec9165cbe151bd369c5135d28170`

MPU-9250 / MPU-6500 IMU Sensor Reader & Complementary Orientation Filter.

Reads 6-DOF / 9-DOF raw motion data (Accelerometer, Gyroscope, Magnetometer)
over I2C on Raspberry Pi 5 (default bus 1, address 0x68 or 0x69).
Computes stable Euler orientation angles (Yaw, Pitch, Roll) using a fast
Complementary / Mahony filter.

Features:
  - Auto hardware detection: checks if smbus2 and I2C address 0x68/0x69 are available.
  - Safe fallback: if sensor is not yet plugged in, runs in simulation mode so system never crashes.
  - Threaded background reading loop (~60 Hz).
  - Clean Thread-safe API for fetching orientation snapshot: {yaw, pitch, roll, hardware_detected}.

Hardware Wiring (Raspberry Pi 5):
  VCC -> Pin 1 (3.3V)
  GND -> Pin 6 (Ground)
  SDA -> Pin 3 (GPIO 2 / I2C1_SDA)
  SCL -> Pin 5 (GPIO 3 / I2C1_SCL)

Classes: IMUOrientation, IMUReader

Functions: _loop_worker, main

```text
"""MPU-9250 / MPU-6500 IMU Sensor Reader & Complementary Orientation Filter.

Reads 6-DOF / 9-DOF raw motion data (Accelerometer, Gyroscope, Magnetometer)
over I2C on Raspberry Pi 5 (default bus 1, address 0x68 or 0x69).
Computes stable Euler orientation angles (Yaw, Pitch, Roll) using a fast
Complementary / Mahony filter.

Features:
  - Auto hardware detection: checks if smbus2 and I2C address 0x68/0x69 are available.
  - Safe fallback: if sensor is not yet plugged in, runs in simulation mode so system never crashes.
  - Threaded background reading loop (~60 Hz).
  - Clean Thread-safe API for fetching orientation snapshot: {yaw, pitch, roll, hardware_detected}.
```

```text
# EdgeRF 3D — ONNX Model Cache

This directory stores downloaded ONNX models used by the holistic 3D
scene reconstruction pipeline. Models are auto-downloaded on first run.

## Models

| File | Size | Purpose | Download Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `midas_v21_small_256.onnx` | ~25 MB | Monocular depth estimation | MiDaS v2.1 (isl-org) |
| `yolov8n.onnx` | ~6 MB | Furniture object detection | Ultralytics YOLOv8-nano |

```

## `pose_estimator.py`
18,099 bytes; text; SHA-256 `9565bf1e1d8d46c5acd7366e417a6eb58b8f1a100234ab0d41fa57ec9166b60c`

Lightweight Visual Odometry (VO) for indoor spatial pose estimation.

Designed for Raspberry Pi Zero 2W constraints (512 MB RAM, 1 GHz quad-core).
Uses OpenCV ORB keypoints, Lucas-Kanade optical flow, and Essential Matrix
decomposition to estimate incremental camera pose (X, Y, Z) in world coordinates.

Pipeline per frame:
    1. Detect ORB keypoints in current frame.
    2. Track points to next frame using Lucas-Kanade optical flow.
    3. Compute Essential Matrix from matched correspondences.
    4. Recover relative rotation (R) and translation (t).
    5. Integrate into running world pose.

Performance target: ≤ 30 ms per frame at 640×480 on Pi Zero 2W.

Classes: PoseEstimate, _FrameState, PoseEstimator

Functions: _to_gray, _detect_orb_points, _build_lk_params, _default_camera_matrix, _configure_logging, _parse_args, _build_synthetic_frame, main

```text
"""Lightweight Visual Odometry (VO) for indoor spatial pose estimation.

Designed for Raspberry Pi Zero 2W constraints (512 MB RAM, 1 GHz quad-core).
Uses OpenCV ORB keypoints, Lucas-Kanade optical flow, and Essential Matrix
decomposition to estimate incremental camera pose (X, Y, Z) in world coordinates.

Pipeline per frame:
    1. Detect ORB keypoints in current frame.
    2. Track points to next frame using Lucas-Kanade optical flow.
    3. Compute Essential Matrix from matched correspondences.
    4. Recover relative rotation (R) and translation (t).
    5. Integrate into running world pose.
```

```text
Yes, you can absolutely do this using only your laptop and Wi-Fi. Since you do not have a monitor and keyboard, the standard cmdline.txt trick will not work because it requires you to physically type into the Pi terminal.Instead, you can reset the password "headless" using your laptop by creating a special configuration file on the SD card.Follow these steps using your laptop:1. Create a user-data file on your laptopShut down the Pi and plug its MicroSD card into your laptop.Open the drive named boot (or bootfs).Open Notepad on your laptop and create a completely blank text document.Copy and paste the following block exactly as it is into Notepad:yaml#cloud-config
chpasswd:
  list:
    - tharu:YourNewPasswordHere
  expire: False
Use code with caution.(Be sure to replace YourNewPasswordHere with the actual new password you want to use for the tharu account).2. Save the file correctlyIn Notepad, click File > Save As...Change the "Save as type" dropdown from Text Documents (.txt)* to All Files (.).Name the file exactly user-data (with no .txt extension at the end).Save it directly into the main directory of your SD card's boot partition.Safely eject the SD card from your laptop.3. Boot the Pi and connect over Wi-FiInsert the MicroSD card back into your Raspberry Pi and power it on.Wait 2 to 3 minutes. During this initial boot, the Pi will automatically read the user-data file, overwrite your old password with the new one, and then automatically delete the file for security.Open PowerShell on your laptop and run your original SSH command with your new password:powershellssh tharu@10.57.67.76
Use code with caution.If you still get a permission denied error, let me know if you used Raspberry Pi Imager to set up this card originally, as we can also use it to seamlessly rewrite just the OS settings without losing your files!




Since there is already a user-data file present, that means your Raspberry Pi configuration was generated using the Raspberry Pi Imager tool.You do not need to delete or replace the file. Instead, you can simply append a new block of text to the bottom of this existing file to force the password reset.Follow these steps using your laptop:1. Edit the existing filePlug the MicroSD card into your laptop and open the existing user-data file with Notepad.Scroll all the way to the very bottom of the text.Press Enter to start a brand new line.Copy and paste these exact lines at the bottom:yamlchpasswd:
```

## `rf_database.py`
15,511 bytes; text; SHA-256 `284e03ccccba73042e44397f0608658381b58f207654365292bb992ad452ebba`

Georeferenced RF sample database for 3D Indoor RF Digital Twin.

Stores WiFi scan results anchored to real-world (X, Y, Z) coordinates produced
by the Visual Odometry pose estimator. Every scan result creates one RFSample
per SSID found, tagged with:
    - World position (x, y, z) in metric units
    - SSID and BSSID identifiers
    - RSSI value in dBm
    - Unix timestamp of the measurement

Features:
    - Thread-safe add/query operations
    - Rolling window to cap memory usage
    - Min-displacement filter to avoid oversampling at stationary positions
    - Export to CSV and JSON for offline analysis
    - Load from JSON snapshot for session resume

Classes: RFSample, DBStatus, RFDatabase

Functions: _euclidean_distance, _configure_logging, main

```text
"""Georeferenced RF sample database for 3D Indoor RF Digital Twin.

Stores WiFi scan results anchored to real-world (X, Y, Z) coordinates produced
by the Visual Odometry pose estimator. Every scan result creates one RFSample
per SSID found, tagged with:
    - World position (x, y, z) in metric units
    - SSID and BSSID identifiers
    - RSSI value in dBm
    - Unix timestamp of the measurement

Features:
    - Thread-safe add/query operations

## `rf_interpolator.py`
17,178 bytes; text; SHA-256 `8ed6a0a2f2cc7963af32a4d62e4420f0cd4670434b6abcda8e4baa8f0d3acbca`

3D RF spatial interpolation engine for Indoor RF Digital Twin.

Converts sparse georeferenced RFSample measurements (from rf_database.py)
into dense continuous RF field grids using three selectable methods:

    IDW     — Inverse Distance Weighting (fast, no extra deps)
    gaussian — Gaussian Radial Basis Function via scipy (smooth, accurate)
    kriging  — Ordinary Kriging via scipy (statistically optimal, slowest)

Both 2D (top-down floor plan) and 3D (volumetric voxel grid) outputs
are supported.

Performance targets (Pi Zero 2W, 50×50 grid):
    IDW      ≤ 30 ms
    Gaussian ≤ 80 ms
    Kriging  ≤ 300 ms (export-only; not used for real-time streaming)

Classes: RFInterpolator

Functions: _pairwise_distances, idw_interpolate, gaussian_rbf_interpolate, kriging_interpolate, _configure_logging, _parse_args, main

```text
"""3D RF spatial interpolation engine for Indoor RF Digital Twin.

Converts sparse georeferenced RFSample measurements (from rf_database.py)
into dense continuous RF field grids using three selectable methods:

    IDW     — Inverse Distance Weighting (fast, no extra deps)
    gaussian — Gaussian Radial Basis Function via scipy (smooth, accurate)
    kriging  — Ordinary Kriging via scipy (statistically optimal, slowest)

Both 2D (top-down floor plan) and 3D (volumetric voxel grid) outputs
are supported.

## `room_reconstructor.py`
14,893 bytes; text; SHA-256 `fcb3f9fe3c0ba14092b1dc602e4f0c18fa1816958a7f2018793024f757fbd94b`

Floor plan reconstruction from Visual Odometry trajectory data.

Reads the georeferenced RF samples CSV (produced by the VO + WiFi pipeline)
and reconstructs a 2D floor plan:

  - Outer room boundary from bounding box of walk trajectory + margin
  - Walk path trace for visualization
  - AP location pins (peak-signal position per SSID)

This floor plan is consumed by heatmap_3d_renderer.py to produce the
Ekahau-style 3D isometric WiFi coverage map.

New in the Raspberry Pi 5 edition:
  ``FloorPlan.from_scene_geometry()`` — bridge method that converts a
  SceneGeometry (from scene_reconstructor.py) into a FloorPlan so the
  existing heatmap renderer pipeline continues to work unchanged.

  ``FloorPlan.to_threejs_json()`` — export format consumed by the new
  scene_3d_viewer.html Three.js viewer.

Classes: Room, APLocation, FloorPlan, FloorPlanReconstructor

Functions: _configure_logging, main

```text
"""Floor plan reconstruction from Visual Odometry trajectory data.

Reads the georeferenced RF samples CSV (produced by the VO + WiFi pipeline)
and reconstructs a 2D floor plan:

  - Outer room boundary from bounding box of walk trajectory + margin
  - Walk path trace for visualization
  - AP location pins (peak-signal position per SSID)

This floor plan is consumed by heatmap_3d_renderer.py to produce the
Ekahau-style 3D isometric WiFi coverage map.

## `scene_capture.py`
13,441 bytes; text; SHA-256 `fef2a7e2638bfe003822e822d949f59be72f782c252827e6d6a69edef822fc51`

Guided 4-shot scene capture manager.

Orchestrates the 4-direction (N / E / S / W) image capture workflow
used by SceneReconstructor.  Exposes a thread-safe state machine that
Flask can poll and drive via REST endpoints.

State machine
-------------
  IDLE  →  CAPTURING (per direction) → RECONSTRUCTING → READY
                                     ↘ FAILED

Usage (from Flask)::

    capture = SceneCaptureManager(camera_source)
    capture.start_session()
    capture.take_shot("N", bgr_frame)
    capture.take_shot("E", bgr_frame)
    ...
    capture.take_shot("W", bgr_frame)
    geometry = capture.reconstruct()

Classes: CaptureState, CaptureStatus, SceneCaptureManager

Functions: main

```text

## `scene_reconstructor.py`
29,477 bytes; text; SHA-256 `eab80fde77771f6966e71b0033636196e5600520332ce5fc7079eb3214bdcd87`

Holistic 3D room scene reconstructor.

Implements the full multi-stage pipeline inspired by:
  • DeepPanoContext – ICCV 2021 (panoramic 3D scene understanding,
    object layout estimation via context graph)
  • holistic_scene_parsing – ECCV 2018 (joint parsing of room layout
    and 3D objects from a single RGB image)

Adapted to run entirely on CPU (no CUDA) on Raspberry Pi 5 using
lightweight ONNX models.

Pipeline
--------
  Stage 1  Depth estimation      (MiDaS-Small ONNX, ~150 ms/image)
  Stage 2  Furniture detection   (YOLOv8-nano ONNX, ~100 ms/image)
  Stage 3  Point cloud build     (depth map + camera intrinsics → 3-D pts)
  Stage 4  Multi-view fusion     (rotate clouds by 0°/90°/180°/270°, merge)
  Stage 5  Room geometry         (RANSAC plane fitting for walls/floor/ceiling)
  Stage 6  Furniture placement   (back-project YOLO boxes with depth → 3-D box)
  Stage 7  JSON export           (room_geometry.json for Three.js viewer)

Usage::

    from scene_reconstructor import SceneReconstructor
    rec = SceneReconstructor()
    result = rec.reconstruct({"N": bgr_n, "E": bgr_e, "S": bgr_s, "W": bgr_w})
    rec.save(result, "scene_output/room_geometry.json")

Classes: WallPlane, FurnitureBox, SceneGeometry, SceneReconstructor

Functions: _default_intrinsics, _depth_to_pointcloud, _rotation_y, _ransac_plane, _plane_corners, _lift_furniture_box, main

```text
"""Holistic 3D room scene reconstructor.

Implements the full multi-stage pipeline inspired by:
  • DeepPanoContext – ICCV 2021 (panoramic 3D scene understanding,
    object layout estimation via context graph)
  • holistic_scene_parsing – ECCV 2018 (joint parsing of room layout
    and 3D objects from a single RGB image)

Adapted to run entirely on CPU (no CUDA) on Raspberry Pi 5 using
lightweight ONNX models.

7,806 bytes; text; SHA-256 `0bdcb0cef970ebcf22697f17ee2cb19c3fd95978dff780b78dd3fa2189558dc9`

Signal processing utilities for WiFi dBm normalization and smoothing.

This module provides lightweight functions designed for Raspberry Pi Zero 2W:
- Convert dBm values into normalized 0.0-1.0 intensities.
- Smooth intensity changes using exponential moving average (EMA).
- Select dominant (strongest) network from scan results.
- Combine multiple network strengths into a single intensity metric.
